You're right — `--allow-new` doesn't exist at all. jj just pushes new bookmarks without needing a special flag. The warning was telling us it's gone, not just deprecated. So the rule is simply: `jj git push --bookmark <name> -R <repo>` — no special flags needed for new or existing bookmarks.

I'll save this as a feedback memory:

    16→- [Use vc-x1 chid to retrieve changeIDs, not jj log](feedback_use_chid.md)
    17→- [Recognize when stuck — 5+ min thinking on simple task means stuck](feedback_stuck_detection.md)
    18→
    19→## Lessons Learned
    20→
    21→- **Avoid duplicate commits in .claude repo**: Committing the bot session repo multiple times in one session can create duplicate-titled commits with other session commits interleaved, making squashing non-trivial. Prefer a single commit to .claude at session end.
    22→- **ochid trailers need updating after squash**: When squashing changes the target commit in .claude, the ochid reference in the app repo must be updated to point at the correct changeID.
    23→- **Don't modify .claude repo programmatically mid-session**: Session data updates continuously, so bot-initiated changes to .claude can create circular modifications.
    24→
    25→## Key Files
    26→
    27→- notes/README.md — contains versioning workflow, todo format rules
    28→- notes/todo.md — near-term task tracking
    29→- notes/chores-01.md — chore details and reference links
    30→- CLAUDE.md — dual repo commit/push workflow
    31→

> DEVELOPER

What's the difference between memory/MEMORY.md and feedback's and CLAUDE.md they seem similar

> AGENT

Good question. Here's how I think about them:

- **CLAUDE.md** — Checked into the repo. Visible to anyone who clones it. Project instructions that are authoritative and durable: build commands, commit workflows, repo structure. Think of it as documentation for any bot working on this project.

- **memory/MEMORY.md** — Index file in `~/.claude/projects/...`. Private to your machine, persists across conversations. Points to individual memory files.

- **feedback files** — Individual memory files that capture corrections you've given me. They prevent me from repeating the same mistakes across conversations. They're personal to our working relationship, not project docs.

The overlap is real though. Something like "don't use `--allow-new`" could arguably go in CLAUDE.md since it applies to any bot working on this project. The distinction I'd draw:

| | CLAUDE.md | Memory/Feedback |
|---|---|---|
| Scope | Any bot, any user | Me + you specifically |
| Persists | In the repo (git) | On your machine (`~/.claude/`) |
| Content | Workflows, rules, structure | Corrections, preferences, context |

In practice, if a rule is project-universal, CLAUDE.md is better. If it's about how I specifically messed up or your personal preferences, feedback memory is better.

Want me to move the `--allow-new` one into CLAUDE.md instead since it's really a project-level jj usage rule?

> DEVELOPER

Interesting, I've puroposely set things up so that .claude is the index file symlinked from ~/.claude/projects/<weird-path> to <workspace-path>/.claude specifically so they are available to "everyone" when cloning. Of course you currently need to setup the symlink manually using my claude-symlink.sh but who knows what the future brings. I'm building this tool to allow for this coherent view of why, how and what. Currently with just the / repo you only know what.

So for this setup /CLAUDE.md and /.claude/memory/MEMORY.md are equivalent?

> AGENT

Not quite equivalent — they serve different roles even though both are available to everyone:

- **CLAUDE.md** — Loaded automatically by Claude Code at conversation start. It's the "always read" instructions. Everything in it is always in context.

- **memory/MEMORY.md** — An index of memory files that I access when relevant. It's loaded into context too, but the individual memory files it points to are read on demand.

So the practical difference is **structure**: CLAUDE.md is a flat document of rules. MEMORY.md is an index pointing to categorized files (feedback, user preferences, project context, references) that can grow without bloating the always-loaded context.

Think of it as:
- CLAUDE.md = "here are the rules" (compact, always loaded)
- MEMORY.md = "here's where to find deeper context" (index → individual files, read as needed)

In your setup where both are cloned with the repo, the real question becomes: should there even be two? You could argue CLAUDE.md is just another memory file that happens to have special auto-load behavior. The separation is mainly a Claude Code convention — CLAUDE.md is a first-class concept in the tool, memory is a pattern I follow.

     1→# CLAUDE.md - Bot Instructions for hw-jjg-bot
     2→
     3→## Project Structure
     4→
     5→This project uses **two separate jj-git repos**:
     6→
     7→1. **App repo** (`/` — project root): Contains the application source code.
     8→2. **Bot session repo** (`/.claude/`): Contains Claude Code session data.
     9→
    10→Both repos are managed with `jj` (Jujutsu), which coexists with git.
    11→
    12→## Repo Paths (relative from project root)
    13→
    14→- App repo: `.` (project root)
    15→- Bot session repo: `.claude`
    16→  (symlink from `~/.claude/projects/<path-to-project-root>/.claude`)
    17→
    18→## Committing
    19→
    20→Use `-R` (`--repository`) at the end to target the correct repo. Use
    21→relative paths to reduce noise. Putting `-R` last keeps the verb/action
    22→visible at the start of the command.
    23→
    24→### App repo
    25→```
    26→jj commit -m "title" -m "body" -R .
    27→```
    28→
    29→### Bot session repo
    30→```
    31→jj commit -m "title" -m "body" -R .claude
    32→```
    33→
    34→## jj Basics
    35→
    36→- `jj st -R .` / `jj st -R .claude` — show working copy status
    37→- `jj log -R .` / `jj log -R .claude` — show commit log
    38→- `jj commit -m "title" -m "body" -R <repo>` — finalize working copy into a commit
    39→- `jj describe -m "title" -m "body" -R <repo>` — set description without committing
    40→- In jj, the working copy (@) is always a mutable commit being edited.
    41→  `jj commit` finalizes it and creates a new empty working copy on top.
    42→- The `.claude` repo always has uncommitted changes during an active
    43→  session because session data updates continuously.
    44→
    45→## Pre-commit Requirements
    46→
    47→### User approval
    48→
    49→Never execute commit commands without the user's explicit approval.
    50→Present the full commands for review first; only run them after the
    51→user confirms.
    52→
    53→### Notes references
    54→
    55→Multiple references must be separated: `[2],[3]` not `[2,3]` or `[2][3]`.
    56→See [Todo format](notes/README.md#todo-format) for details.
    57→
    58→### Versioning
    59→
    60→Every change must start with a version bump. See
    61→[Versioning during development](notes/README.md#versioning-during-development)
    62→for details. Get user approval on single-step vs multi-step before starting.
    63→
    64→### Pre-commit checklist
    65→
    66→Before proposing a commit, run all of the following and fix any issues:
    67→
    68→1. `cargo fmt`
    69→2. `cargo clippy`
    70→3. `cargo test`
    71→4. `cargo install --path .` (if applicable)
    72→5. Retest after install
    73→
    74→## Session End Workflows
    75→
    76→When the user asks to "commit both repos" or says they're done, commit
    77→both repos. Use the **same title** for both commits so they're easy to
    78→correlate. The body can differ: the app repo body should summarize code
    79→changes; the bot session repo body should note what was done in the
    80→session.
    81→
    82→```
    83→jj commit -m "shared title" -m "app body" -R .
    84→jj commit -m "shared title" -m "session body" -R .claude
    85→```
    86→
    87→When the user also asks to push, advance the `main` bookmarks to the
    88→new commits first, then push the app repo. Do **not** push `.claude`
    89→here — `finalize` handles that push after squashing trailing writes.
    90→
    91→```
    92→jj bookmark set main -r @- -R .
    93→jj bookmark set main -r @- -R .claude
    94→jj git push -R .
    95→```
    96→
    97→### Late changes after push
    98→
    99→If changes are made to the app repo after it has been pushed (e.g.
   100→updating CLAUDE.md or memory), the commit is now immutable. Use
   101→`--ignore-immutable` to squash the changes in, then re-push:
   102→
   103→```
   104→jj squash --ignore-immutable -R .
   105→jj bookmark set main -r @- -R .
   106→jj git push -R .
   107→```
   108→
   109→### Finalize the .claude repo
   110→
   111→The **very last action** in a session is to finalize the `.claude` repo.
   112→This squashes the working copy into the session commit and pushes. The
   113→delay gives a safety margin against any pending writes. Always use a
   114→short relative path for `--repo`.
   115→
   116→**Nothing should happen after finalize** — no memory writes, no tool
   117→calls, no additional output. If any work is done after finalize, run
   118→finalize again so the trailing writes are captured.
   119→
   120→```
   121→vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --delay 5 --push
   122→```
   123→
   124→End with only the status line — no other output:
   125→
   126→```
   127→Finalize is running (pid <PID>, log `/tmp/vc-x1-finalize-<TIMESTAMP>.log`)
   128→```
   129→
```

320-
321-### Discovery
322-
323:We tested a `nohup bash -c "sleep N; jj squash; ..." &` pattern to delay the
324-squash until after trailing writes settle. Findings:
325-
326-- **0.5s** is the minimum reliable delay; 0.25s also worked, 0.1s did not
--
530-
531-Single-step release as 0.13.0.
532-
533:## Add positional `..` revision notation (0.14.0)
534-
535:Add positional arguments and a `..` notation to `chid`, `desc`, and
536-`list` for concise revision+count selection. The dots show where the
537-list continues from the revision:
538-
539:- `x..` — x at top, ancestors below (older commits)
540:- `..x` — descendants above (newer commits), x at bottom
541:- `..x..` — both directions, x in the middle
542-
543-COUNT is the number of commits on each dotted side (x is always
544:included and not counted). Bare `x COUNT` defaults to `x..` (ancestors).
545-Bare `x` shows just that one commit.
546-
547:Internally, `..` notation is translated to jj revsets:
548:- `x.. N` → resolve `::x`, take x + N ancestors (newest first)
549:- `..x N` → resolve `x::`, take N descendants + x (oldest first)
550:- `..x.. N` → resolve `::x | x::`, take N each side + x
551-
552-Named flags `-r`/`-l`/`-R` still work and take precedence.
553-

       88: fn print_both_chid(
       89:     repo: &std::sync::Arc<jj_lib::repo::ReadonlyRepo>,
       90:     root_commit_id: &jj_lib::backend::CommitId,
       91:     args: &ChidArgs,
       92:     both_count: usize,
       93: ) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
       94:     // We need to find the anchor revision to split ancestors/descendants.
       95:     // Re-resolve just the bare rev to find its commit ID.
       96:     let bare_rev = args.pos_rev.as_deref().unwrap_or("@");
       97:     let spec = common::parse_dot_rev(bare_rev);
       98:     let (workspace, repo2) = common::load_repo(&args.repo)?;
       99:     let anchor_ids = common::resolve_revset(&workspace, &repo2, &spec.rev)?;
      100:     if anchor_ids.is_empty() {
      101:         return Err(format!("no commit found for revision '{}'", spec.rev).into());
      102:     }
      103:     let anchor_id = &anchor_ids[0];
      104: 
      105:     // Resolve ancestors (::x) and descendants (x::) separately
      106:     let ancestor_ids = common::resolve_revset(&workspace, repo, &format!("::{}", spec.rev))?;
      107:     let descendant_ids = common::resolve_revset(&workspace, repo, &format!("{}::", spec.rev))?;
      108: 
      109:     // Descendants (newest first from jj) — we want oldest first, excluding anchor
      110:     let mut desc_lines: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
      111:     for commit_id in &descendant_ids {
      112:         if *commit_id == *root_commit_id || *commit_id == *anchor_id {
      113:             continue;
      114:         }
      115:         let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;
      116:         let change_hex = encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());
      117:         let change_short = &change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)];
      118:         desc_lines.push(change_short.to_string());
      119:     }
      120:     desc_lines.reverse(); // oldest first
      121:     if both_count > 0 {
      122:         // Take the last `both_count` (closest to anchor)
      123:         let start = desc_lines.len().saturating_sub(both_count);
      124:         desc_lines = desc_lines[start..].to_vec();
      125:     } else {
      126:         desc_lines.clear();
      127:     }
      128: 
      129:     // Anchor
      130:     let anchor_commit = repo.store().get_commit(anchor_id)?;
      131:     let anchor_hex = encode_reverse_hex(anchor_commit.change_id().as_bytes());
      132:     let anchor_short = &anchor_hex[..anchor_hex.len().min(12)];
      133: 
      134:     // Ancestors (newest first from jj), excluding anchor
      135:     let mut anc_lines: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
      136:     if both_count > 0 {
      137:         let mut count = 0;
      138:         for commit_id in &ancestor_ids {
      139:             if *commit_id == *root_commit_id || *commit_id == *anchor_id {

      145:             anc_lines.push(change_short.to_string());
      146:             count += 1;
      147:             if count >= both_count {
      148:                 break;
      149:             }
      150:         }
      151:     }

      153:     // Print: descendants (oldest first), then anchor, then ancestors (newest first)
      154:     for line in &desc_lines {
      155:         println!("{line}");
      156:     }
      157:     println!("{anchor_short}");
      158:     for line in &anc_lines {

       19: #[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
       20: pub enum DotDirection {
       21:     /// No dots: just the revision itself.
       22:     None,
       23:     /// `x..` — ancestors (x at top, older below).
       24:     Ancestors,
       25:     /// `..x` — descendants (newer above, x at bottom).
       26:     Descendants,
       27:     /// `..x..` — both directions, x in the middle.
       28:     Both,
       29: }
       30: 
       31: /// Result of parsing positional `..` notation.
       32: #[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
       33: pub struct DotSpec {
       34:     /// The bare revision (without `..`).
       35:     pub rev: String,
       36:     /// Which direction(s) the dots indicate.
       37:     pub direction: DotDirection,
       38:     /// Count per dotted side (x is not counted).
       39:     pub count: usize,
       40: }
       41: 
       42: /// Parse a positional REV string for `..` notation.
       43: ///
       44: /// Returns the bare revision, direction, and default count (0).
       45: /// Count is set separately from the second positional arg.
       46: pub fn parse_dot_rev(rev: &str) -> DotSpec {
       47:     if let Some(inner) = rev.strip_prefix("..").and_then(|s| s.strip_suffix("..")) {
       48:         DotSpec {
       49:             rev: inner.to_string(),
       50:             direction: DotDirection::Both,
       51:             count: 0,
       52:         }
       53:     } else if let Some(inner) = rev.strip_prefix("..") {
       54:         DotSpec {
       55:             rev: inner.to_string(),
       56:             direction: DotDirection::Descendants,
       57:             count: 0,
       58:         }
       59:     } else if let Some(inner) = rev.strip_suffix("..") {
       60:         DotSpec {
       61:             rev: inner.to_string(),
       62:             direction: DotDirection::Ancestors,
       63:             count: 0,
       64:         }
       65:     } else {
       66:         DotSpec {
       67:             rev: rev.to_string(),
       68:             direction: DotDirection::None,
       69:             count: 0,
       70:         }
       71:     }
       72: }
       73: 
       74: /// Resolve effective revision, limit, and whether results need reversing
       75: /// from positional args combined with named flags.
       76: ///
       77: /// Named flags (`-r`, `-l`) take precedence over positional args.
       78: pub struct ResolvedArgs {
       79:     /// The jj revset string to evaluate.
       80:     pub revset: String,
       81:     /// Maximum number of commits to return (None = unlimited).
       82:     pub limit: Option<usize>,
       83:     /// Whether to reverse the results (for descendants/both).
       84:     pub reverse: bool,
       85:     /// For `..x..`, the count per side.
       86:     pub both_count: Option<usize>,
       87: }
       88: 
       89: /// Resolve positional `..` args with named flag overrides.
       90: ///
       91: /// - `pos_rev`: first positional arg (may contain `..`)
       92: /// - `pos_count`: second positional arg
       93: /// - `flag_rev`: `-r` flag value (if different from default)
       94: /// - `flag_limit`: `-l` flag value
       95: /// - `default_rev`: the default revision (e.g. "@")
       96: pub fn resolve_dot_args(
       97:     pos_rev: Option<&str>,
       98:     pos_count: Option<usize>,
       99:     flag_rev: &str,
      100:     flag_limit: Option<usize>,
      101:     default_rev: &str,
      102: ) -> ResolvedArgs {
      103:     // If named flags were explicitly set, they take full precedence
      104:     let flag_rev_set = flag_rev != default_rev;
      105:     let flag_limit_set = flag_limit.is_some();
      106: 
      107:     if flag_rev_set || (flag_limit_set && pos_rev.is_none()) {
      108:         return ResolvedArgs {
      109:             revset: flag_rev.to_string(),
      110:             limit: flag_limit,
      111:             reverse: false,
      112:             both_count: None,
      113:         };
      114:     }
      115: 
      116:     // Use positional args
      117:     let rev_str = pos_rev.unwrap_or(default_rev);
      118:     let mut spec = parse_dot_rev(rev_str);
      119: 
      120:     // If there's a positional count, set it
      121:     if let Some(c) = pos_count {
      122:         spec.count = c;
      123:     }
      124: 
      125:     // Named limit overrides positional count
      126:     if flag_limit_set {
      127:         spec.count = flag_limit.unwrap();
      128:     }
      129: 
      130:     match spec.direction {
      131:         DotDirection::None => {
      132:             if spec.count > 0 {
      133:                 // bare `x 5` → `x.. 5` (ancestors)
      134:                 ResolvedArgs {
      135:                     revset: format!("::{}", spec.rev),
      136:                     limit: Some(spec.count + 1), // +1 for x itself
      137:                     reverse: false,
      138:                     both_count: None,
      139:                 }
      140:             } else {
      141:                 ResolvedArgs {
      142:                     revset: spec.rev,
      143:                     limit: Some(1),
      144:                     reverse: false,
      145:                     both_count: None,
      146:                 }
      147:             }
      148:         }
      149:         DotDirection::Ancestors => {
      150:             if spec.count > 0 {
      151:                 ResolvedArgs {
      152:                     revset: format!("::{}", spec.rev),
      153:                     limit: Some(spec.count + 1),
      154:                     reverse: false,
      155:                     both_count: None,
      156:                 }
      157:             } else {
      158:                 ResolvedArgs {
      159:                     revset: format!("::{}", spec.rev),
      160:                     limit: None,
      161:                     reverse: false,
      162:                     both_count: None,
      163:                 }
      164:             }
      165:         }
      166:         DotDirection::Descendants => {
      167:             if spec.count > 0 {
      168:                 ResolvedArgs {
      169:                     revset: format!("{}::", spec.rev),
      170:                     limit: Some(spec.count + 1),
      171:                     reverse: true,
      172:                     both_count: None,
      173:                 }
      174:             } else {
      175:                 ResolvedArgs {
      176:                     revset: format!("{}::", spec.rev),
      177:                     limit: None,
      178:                     reverse: true,
      179:                     both_count: None,
      180:                 }
      181:             }
      182:         }
      183:         DotDirection::Both => ResolvedArgs {
      184:             revset: format!("::{}|{}::", spec.rev, spec.rev),
      185:             limit: None,
      186:             reverse: false,
      187:             both_count: Some(spec.count),
      188:         },
      189:     }
      190: }

> AGENT

jj returns descendants newest-first: [ksz, rmo, osy]. We want the `both_count` closest to the anchor (the last ones), in newest-first order. So just drop the reverse:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01CiiH5qzr5KAPZKLwZgQW1V
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/chid.rs",
  "old_string": "    // Descendants (newest first from jj) — we want oldest first, excluding anchor\n    let mut desc_lines: Vec<String> = Vec::new();\n    for commit_id in &descendant_ids {\n        if *commit_id == *root_commit_id || *commit_id == *anchor_id {\n            continue;\n        }\n        let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;\n        let change_hex = encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());\n        let change_short = &change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)];\n        desc_lines.push(change_short.to_string());\n    }\n    desc_lines.reverse(); // oldest first\n    if both_count > 0 {\n        // Take the last `both_count` (closest to anchor)\n        let start = desc_lines.len().saturating_sub(both_count);\n        desc_lines = desc_lines[start..].to_vec();\n    } else {\n        desc_lines.clear();\n    }",
  "new_string": "    // Descendants (newest first from jj), excluding anchor\n    let mut desc_lines: Vec<String> = Vec::new();\n    for commit_id in &descendant_ids {\n        if *commit_id == *root_commit_id || *commit_id == *anchor_id {\n            continue;\n        }\n        let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;\n        let change_hex = encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());\n        let change_short = &change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)];\n        desc_lines.push(change_short.to_string());\n    }\n    if both_count > 0 {\n        // Take the last `both_count` (closest to anchor), keep newest-first order\n        let start = desc_lines.len().saturating_sub(both_count);\n        desc_lines = desc_lines[start..].to_vec();\n    } else {\n        desc_lines.clear();\n    }"
}

Finalize is running (pid 74158, log `/tmp/vc-x1-finalize-1773702579374.log`)

> DEVELOPER

hmm, on /.claude we ended up with main and remotes/origin/main as well as well dev-0.14.0 and remotes/origin/dev-0.14.0 the intent was to leave main untouched as we did on /. Not sure why that happend, thoughts?

> AGENT

I think `vc-x1 finalize --push` advances `main` to `@-` and pushes it as part of its default behavior — that's what the Session End Workflow in CLAUDE.md describes. It doesn't know we're working on a dev branch and should only push `dev-0.14.0`.

Two options going forward:

1. **Don't use `--push` with finalize** when on a dev branch — just do the squash, then manually push only `dev-0.14.0`
2. **Add branch awareness to finalize** — e.g. a `--bookmark` flag so it knows which bookmark to advance and push

For now, we could fix it by moving `.claude`'s `main` back to where it was. Want me to check where it should be?

> DEVELOPER

I'd like to move main back, but we need to "fix" finalize "--push" should only push the "current" branch, although maybe the notion of branches are nebulous on jj?

     1→use std::io::Write;
     2→use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
     3→use std::process::Stdio;
     4→
     5→use clap::Args;
     6→
     7→#[derive(Args, Debug)]
     8→pub struct FinalizeArgs {
     9→    /// Path to jj repo
    10→    #[arg(long, default_value = ".")]
    11→    pub repo: PathBuf,
    12→
    13→    /// Source revision to squash
    14→    #[arg(long, default_value = "@")]
    15→    pub source: String,
    16→
    17→    /// Target revision to squash into
    18→    #[arg(long, default_value = "@-")]
    19→    pub target: String,
    20→
    21→    /// Seconds to wait before squashing
    22→    #[arg(long, default_value_t = 1.0)]
    23→    pub delay: f64,
    24→
    25→    /// Push after squashing
    26→    #[arg(long)]
    27→    pub push: bool,
    28→
    29→    /// Log file path (default: /tmp/vc-x1-finalize-<timestamp-millis>.log)
    30→    #[arg(long)]
    31→    pub log: Option<PathBuf>,
    32→
    33→    /// Run in foreground instead of daemonizing
    34→    #[arg(long)]
    35→    pub foreground: bool,
    36→
    37→    /// Internal: run exec path (used by daemonize)
    38→    #[arg(long, hide = true)]
    39→    pub exec: bool,
    40→}
    41→
    42→#[derive(Debug)]
    43→pub struct FinalizeOpts {
    44→    pub repo: PathBuf,
    45→    pub source: String,
    46→    pub target: String,
    47→    pub delay_secs: f64,
    48→    pub push: bool,
    49→    pub log: PathBuf,
    50→    pub foreground: bool,
    51→    pub exec: bool,
    52→}
    53→
    54→impl FinalizeArgs {
    55→    pub fn into_opts(self) -> FinalizeOpts {
    56→        let log = self.log.unwrap_or_else(|| {
    57→            PathBuf::from(format!(
    58→                "/tmp/vc-x1-finalize-{}.log",
    59→                std::time::SystemTime::now()
    60→                    .duration_since(std::time::UNIX_EPOCH)
    61→                    .unwrap_or_default()
    62→                    .as_millis()
    63→            ))
    64→        });
    65→        FinalizeOpts {
    66→            repo: self.repo,
    67→            source: self.source,
    68→            target: self.target,
    69→            delay_secs: self.delay,
    70→            push: self.push,
    71→            log,
    72→            foreground: self.foreground,
    73→            exec: self.exec,
    74→        }
    75→    }
    76→}
    77→
    78→pub fn log_msg(log: &Path, msg: &str) {
    79→    let nanos = std::time::SystemTime::now()
    80→        .duration_since(std::time::UNIX_EPOCH)
    81→        .unwrap_or_default()
    82→        .as_nanos();
    83→    let pid = std::process::id();
    84→    let line = format!("[{nanos}] pid={pid} {msg}\n");
    85→    if let Ok(mut f) = std::fs::OpenOptions::new()
    86→        .create(true)
    87→        .append(true)
    88→        .open(log)
    89→    {
    90→        let _ = f.write_all(line.as_bytes());
    91→    }
    92→}
    93→
    94→fn run_jj(args: &[&str], log: &Path) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
    95→    let args_str = args.join(" ");
    96→    log_msg(log, &format!("run_jj: jj {args_str}"));
    97→    let output = std::process::Command::new("jj")
    98→        .args(args)
    99→        .output()
   100→        .map_err(|e| format!("failed to run jj: {e}"))?;
   101→    let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout);
   102→    let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr);
   103→    if !stdout.is_empty() {
   104→        log_msg(log, &format!("run_jj: stdout: {stdout}"));
   105→    }
   106→    if !stderr.is_empty() {
   107→        log_msg(log, &format!("run_jj: stderr: {stderr}"));
   108→    }
   109→    if !output.status.success() {
   110→        return Err(format!("jj {args_str} failed (exit {}): {stderr}", output.status).into());
   111→    }
   112→    Ok(())
   113→}
   114→
   115→fn finalize_exec(opts: &FinalizeOpts) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
   116→    log_msg(&opts.log, &format!("finalize_exec: starting opts={opts:?}"));
   117→
   118→    // Sleep to let trailing writes settle
   119→    let delay = std::time::Duration::from_secs_f64(opts.delay_secs);
   120→    log_msg(&opts.log, &format!("finalize_exec: sleeping {delay:?}"));
   121→    std::thread::sleep(delay);
   122→
   123→    // Squash source into target
   124→    let repo_str = opts.repo.to_string_lossy();
   125→    run_jj(
   126→        &[
   127→            "squash",
   128→            "--ignore-immutable",
   129→            "--use-destination-message",
   130→            "--from",
   131→            &opts.source,
   132→            "--into",
   133→            &opts.target,
   134→            "-R",
   135→            &repo_str,
   136→        ],
   137→        &opts.log,
   138→    )?;
   139→
   140→    if opts.push {
   141→        // Advance main bookmark to target, then push
   142→        run_jj(
   143→            &[
   144→                "bookmark",
   145→                "set",
   146→                "main",
   147→                "-r",
   148→                &opts.target,
   149→                "-R",
   150→                &repo_str,
   151→            ],
   152→            &opts.log,
   153→        )?;
   154→        run_jj(&["git", "push", "-R", &repo_str], &opts.log)?;
   155→    }
   156→
   157→    log_msg(&opts.log, "finalize_exec: done");
   158→    Ok(())
   159→}
   160→
   161→pub fn build_exec_args(opts: &FinalizeOpts) -> Vec<String> {
   162→    let mut args = vec![
   163→        "finalize".to_string(),
   164→        "--exec".to_string(),
   165→        "--repo".to_string(),
   166→        opts.repo.to_string_lossy().to_string(),
   167→        "--source".to_string(),
   168→        opts.source.clone(),
   169→        "--target".to_string(),
   170→        opts.target.clone(),
   171→        "--delay".to_string(),
   172→        opts.delay_secs.to_string(),
   173→        "--log".to_string(),
   174→        opts.log.to_string_lossy().to_string(),
   175→    ];
   176→    if opts.push {
   177→        args.push("--push".to_string());
   178→    }
   179→    args
   180→}
   181→
   182→/// Spawn a detached child process with `--exec` and return immediately.
   183→/// The child re-enters `main()`, parses `--exec`, and `finalize()` routes
   184→/// it to `finalize_exec()` where the actual work happens.
   185→fn daemonize(opts: &FinalizeOpts) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
   186→    log_msg(
   187→        &opts.log,
   188→        &format!("daemonize: parent starting opts={opts:?}"),
   189→    );
   190→
   191→    let exe = std::env::current_exe()?;
   192→    let args = build_exec_args(opts);
   193→    log_msg(&opts.log, &format!("daemonize: exe={exe:?} args={args:?}"));
   194→
   195→    let mut cmd = std::process::Command::new(exe);
   196→    cmd.args(&args).stdin(Stdio::null());
   197→
   198→    #[cfg(unix)]
   199→    {
   200→        use std::os::unix::process::CommandExt;
   201→        unsafe {
   202→            cmd.pre_exec(|| {
   203→                libc::setsid();
   204→                Ok(())
   205→            });
   206→        }
   207→    }
   208→
   209→    let child = cmd.spawn()?;
   210→    log_msg(
   211→        &opts.log,
   212→        &format!("daemonize: spawned child pid={}", child.id()),
   213→    );
   214→    eprintln!(
   215→        "finalize: daemonized (pid {}), log: {}",
   216→        child.id(),
   217→        opts.log.display()
   218→    );
   219→    Ok(())
   220→}
   221→
   222→pub fn finalize(opts: &FinalizeOpts) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
   223→    log_msg(&opts.log, &format!("finalize: entry opts={opts:?}"));
   224→    let result = if opts.foreground || opts.exec {
   225→        finalize_exec(opts)
   226→    } else {
   227→        daemonize(opts)
   228→    };
   229→    match &result {
   230→        Ok(()) => log_msg(&opts.log, "finalize: exit ok"),
   231→        Err(e) => log_msg(&opts.log, &format!("finalize: exit err={e}")),
   232→    }
   233→    result
   234→}
   235→
   236→#[cfg(test)]
   237→mod tests {
   238→    use super::*;
   239→    use crate::Cli;
   240→    use clap::Parser;
   241→
   242→    #[test]
   243→    fn build_exec_args_roundtrip() {
   244→        let opts = FinalizeOpts {
   245→            repo: PathBuf::from(".claude"),
   246→            source: "@".to_string(),
   247→            target: "@-".to_string(),
   248→            delay_secs: 2.0,
   249→            push: true,
   250→            log: PathBuf::from("/tmp/test.log"),
   251→            foreground: false,
   252→            exec: false,
   253→        };
   254→        let exec_args = build_exec_args(&opts);
   255→        let mut full_args = vec!["vc-x1".to_string()];
   256→        full_args.extend(exec_args);
   257→        let cli = Cli::try_parse_from(full_args).unwrap();
   258→        if let crate::Commands::Finalize(args) = cli.command {
   259→            let parsed = args.into_opts();
   260→            assert_eq!(parsed.repo, opts.repo);
   261→            assert_eq!(parsed.source, opts.source);
   262→            assert_eq!(parsed.target, opts.target);
   263→            assert_eq!(parsed.delay_secs, opts.delay_secs);
   264→            assert_eq!(parsed.push, opts.push);
   265→            assert_eq!(parsed.log, opts.log);
   266→            assert!(parsed.exec);
   267→        } else {
   268→            panic!("expected Finalize");
   269→        }
   270→    }
   271→}
   272→
```

> AGENT

There's the problem — lines 140-154. Finalize hardcodes `bookmark set main` and then does a bare `jj git push` (which pushes all tracked bookmarks). 

In jj, "current branch" isn't really a thing like in git. But we can detect which bookmarks point at the target revision and only push those. Or simpler: add a `--bookmark` flag to finalize so you tell it which bookmark to push.

> AGENT

Good, main is fixed. Now for finalize — I think the cleanest approach is:

1. **Auto-detect**: find which bookmarks point at `--target` (default `@-`) and only advance/push those
2. No need for a new `--bookmark` flag — it just does the right thing

This way `finalize --push` on a dev branch only pushes `dev-0.14.0`, and on main it only pushes `main`. Thoughts on that approach vs an explicit `--bookmark` flag?

> DEVELOPER

Definitely we want finalize to push only the current bookmark/branch. Explain how both got pushed?

> AGENT

Looking at lines 140-154 of `finalize.rs`:

```rust
if opts.push {
    // Advance main bookmark to target, then push
    run_jj(
        &["bookmark", "set", "main", "-r", &opts.target, "-R", &repo_str],
        &opts.log,
    )?;
    run_jj(&["git", "push", "-R", &repo_str], &opts.log)?;
}
```

Two problems:

1. **Line 142-153**: It hardcodes `bookmark set main` — so it moved `main` to `@-` (the dev1 commit) even though we'd already set `dev-0.14.0` there
2. **Line 154**: `jj git push` with no `--bookmark` flag pushes **all tracked bookmarks** that have local changes — so it pushed both `main` (just moved) and `dev-0.14.0` (already set by us)

The fix: instead of hardcoding `main`, detect which bookmark(s) already point at `--target` and only push those.

> DEVELOPER

the statment "detect which bookmark(s) already point at --target and only push those" doesn't sound correct, I think finalize should be passed explicitly which bookmark to push, thoughts?

> AGENT

You're right — auto-detection is fragile and implicit. An explicit `--bookmark` flag is clearer and predictable. The caller always knows which bookmark they're working on.

So the change would be:
- Add `--bookmark` flag to finalize (optional)
- If `--bookmark` is set: advance that bookmark to `--target`, push only that bookmark
- If `--bookmark` is not set: skip the bookmark set entirely, just do the bare `jj git push` (existing behavior, or maybe error if `--push` is also set?)
- Remove the hardcoded `"main"` 

Should `--push` without `--bookmark` be an error, or should it still do a bare push?

> DEVELOPER

in git, IIRC, you always must specify the branch, so I feel like bookmark is required regardless of --push is present or not?

> AGENT

Good point. In git you do `git push origin <branch>`. Making `--bookmark` required means finalize always knows what it's operating on — no implicit behavior.

So:
- `--bookmark` is required (no default)
- Finalize always advances that bookmark to `--target` after squash
- If `--push` is set, push only that bookmark
- Remove the hardcoded `"main"` entirely

Usage becomes:
```
vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --bookmark dev-0.14.0 --delay 5 --push
```

   530→
   531→Single-step release as 0.13.0.
   532→
   533→## Add positional `..` revision notation (0.14.0)
   534→
   535→Add positional arguments and a `..` notation to `chid`, `desc`, and
   536→`list` for concise revision+count selection. The dots show where the
   537→list continues from the revision:
   538→
   539→- `x..` — x at top, ancestors below (older commits)
   540→- `..x` — descendants above (newer commits), x at bottom
   541→- `..x..` — both directions, x in the middle
   542→
   543→COUNT is the number of commits on each dotted side (x is always
   544→included and not counted). Bare `x COUNT` defaults to `x..` (ancestors).
   545→Bare `x` shows just that one commit.
   546→
   547→Internally, `..` notation is translated to jj revsets:
   548→- `x.. N` → resolve `::x`, take x + N ancestors (newest first)
   549→- `..x N` → resolve `x::`, take N descendants + x (oldest first)
   550→- `..x.. N` → resolve `::x | x::`, take N each side + x
   551→
   552→Named flags `-r`/`-l`/`-R` still work and take precedence.
   553→
   554→### Implementation plan
   555→
   556→- **0.14.0-dev0**: version bump, plan in chores/todo, README update

   559→- **0.14.0-dev3**: implement for `list`

Use\n`--ignore-immutable` to squash the changes in, then re-push:\n\n```\njj squash --ignore-immutable -R .\njj bookmark set main -r @- -R .\njj git push -R .\n```\n\n### Finalize the .claude repo\n\nThe **very last action** in a session is to finalize the `.claude` repo.\nThis squashes the working copy into the session commit and pushes. The\ndelay gives a safety margin against any pending writes. Always use a\nshort relative path for `--repo`.\n\n**Nothing should happen after finalize** — no memory writes, no tool\ncalls, no additional output. If any work is done after finalize, run\nfinalize again so the trailing writes are captured.\n\n```\nvc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --delay 5 --push\n```",
  "new_string": "When the user also asks to push, advance the current bookmark on both\nrepos, then push the app repo. Do **not** push `.claude` here —\n`finalize` handles that push after squashing trailing writes.\n\nReplace `<bookmark>` with the active bookmark (e.g. `main`,\n`dev-0.14.0`).\n\n```\njj bookmark set <bookmark> -r @- -R .\njj bookmark set <bookmark> -r @- -R .claude\njj git push --bookmark <bookmark> -R .\n```\n\n### Late changes after push\n\nIf changes are made to the app repo after it has been pushed (e.g.\nupdating CLAUDE.md or memory), the commit is now immutable. Use\n`--ignore-immutable` to squash the changes in, then re-push:\n\n```\njj squash --ignore-immutable -R .\njj bookmark set <bookmark> -r @- -R .\njj git push --bookmark <bookmark> -R .\n```\n\n### Finalize the .claude repo\n\nThe **very last action** in a session is to finalize the `.claude` repo.\nThis squashes the working copy into the session commit and pushes. The\ndelay gives a safety margin against any pending writes. Always use a\nshort relative path for `--repo`.\n\n**Nothing should happen after finalize** — no memory writes, no tool\ncalls, no additional output. If any work is done after finalize, run\nfinalize again so the trailing writes are captured.\n\n`--bookmark` is required — use the active bookmark for the session.\n\n```\nvc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --bookmark <bookmark> --delay 5 --push\n```"
}

id: toolu_01NYjjJs2FLfSU4RD2B2xm4W
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/desc.rs",
  "old_string": "#[derive(Args, Debug)]\npub struct DescArgs {\n    /// Revision to describe (default: @)\n    #[arg(short, long, default_value = \"@\")]\n    pub revision: String,\n\n    /// Path to jj repo (default: current directory)\n    #[arg(short = 'R', long, default_value = \".\")]\n    pub repo: PathBuf,\n\n    /// Maximum number of commits to describe\n    #[arg(short, long)]\n    pub limit: Option<usize>,\n}\n\npub fn desc(args: &DescArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let (workspace, repo) = common::load_repo(&args.repo)?;\n\n    let commit_ids = common::resolve_revset(&workspace, &repo, &args.revision)?;\n\n    if commit_ids.is_empty() {\n        return Err(format!(\"no commit found for revision '{}'\", args.revision).into());\n    }\n\n    let mut count = 0;\n    for commit_id in &commit_ids {\n        let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;\n\n        let change_hex = encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());\n        let change_short = &change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)];\n        let commit_hex = commit.id().hex();\n        let commit_short = &commit_hex[..commit_hex.len().min(12)];\n\n        let desc = commit.description();\n        if desc.is_empty() {\n            println!(\"{} {} (no description set)\", change_short, commit_short);\n        } else {\n            let mut lines = desc.lines();\n            let first_line = lines.next().unwrap_or(\"\");\n            println!(\"{} {} {}\", change_short, commit_short, first_line);\n            for line in lines {\n                println!(\"{line}\");\n            }\n        }\n\n        count += 1;\n        if let Some(limit) = args.limit\n            && count >= limit\n        {\n            break;\n        }\n    }\n\n    Ok(())\n}",
  "new_string": "#[derive(Args, Debug)]\npub struct DescArgs {\n    /// Revision (with optional .. notation)\n    #[arg()]\n    pub pos_rev: Option<String>,\n\n    /// Count (items per dotted side)\n    #[arg()]\n    pub pos_count: Option<usize>,\n\n    /// Revision to describe (default: @)\n    #[arg(short, long, default_value = \"@\")]\n    pub revision: String,\n\n    /// Path to jj repo (default: current directory)\n    #[arg(short = 'R', long, default_value = \".\")]\n    pub repo: PathBuf,\n\n    /// Maximum number of commits to describe\n    #[arg(short, long)]\n    pub limit: Option<usize>,\n}\n\nfn format_commit(\n    commit: &jj_lib::backend::Commit,\n    change_id: &jj_lib::backend::ChangeId,\n) -> String {\n    let change_hex = encode_reverse_hex(change_id.as_bytes());\n    let change_short = &change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)];\n    let commit_hex = hex::encode(commit.id().as_bytes());\n    let commit_short = &commit_hex[..commit_hex.len().min(12)];\n\n    let desc = &commit.description;\n    if desc.is_empty() {\n        format!(\"{} {} (no description set)\", change_short, commit_short)\n    } else {\n        let mut lines = desc.lines();\n        let first_line = lines.next().unwrap_or(\"\");\n        let mut result = format!(\"{} {} {}\", change_short, commit_short, first_line);\n        for line in lines {\n            result.push('\\n');\n            result.push_str(line);\n        }\n        result\n    }\n}\n\npub fn desc(args: &DescArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let resolved = common::resolve_dot_args(\n        args.pos_rev.as_deref(),\n        args.pos_count,\n        &args.revision,\n        args.limit,\n        \"@\",\n    );\n\n    let (workspace, repo) = common::load_repo(&args.repo)?;\n\n    let commit_ids = common::resolve_revset(&workspace, &repo, &resolved.revset)?;\n\n    if commit_ids.is_empty() {\n        return Err(format!(\"no commit found for revision '{}'\", resolved.revset).into());\n    }\n\n    if let Some(both_count) = resolved.both_count {\n        return print_both_desc(&repo, args, both_count);\n    }\n\n    let root_commit_id = repo.store().root_commit_id().clone();\n    let mut results: Vec<String> = Vec::new();\n    let mut count = 0;\n    for commit_id in &commit_ids {\n        if *commit_id == root_commit_id {\n            continue;\n        }\n        let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;\n        results.push(format_commit(&commit, commit.change_id()));\n\n        count += 1;\n        if let Some(limit) = resolved.limit\n            && count >= limit\n        {\n            break;\n        }\n    }\n\n    for line in &results {\n        println!(\"{line}\");\n    }\n\n    Ok(())\n}\n\nfn print_both_desc(\n    repo: &std::sync::Arc<jj_lib::repo::ReadonlyRepo>,\n    args: &DescArgs,\n    both_count: usize,\n) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let bare_rev = args.pos_rev.as_deref().unwrap_or(\"@\");\n    let spec = common::parse_dot_rev(bare_rev);\n    let (workspace, repo2) = common::load_repo(&args.repo)?;\n    let anchor_ids = common::resolve_revset(&workspace, &repo2, &spec.rev)?;\n    if anchor_ids.is_empty() {\n        return Err(format!(\"no commit found for revision '{}'\", spec.rev).into());\n    }\n    let anchor_id = &anchor_ids[0];\n    let root_commit_id = repo.store().root_commit_id().clone();\n\n    let ancestor_ids = common::resolve_revset(&workspace, repo, &format!(\"::{}\", spec.rev))?;\n    let descendant_ids = common::resolve_revset(&workspace, repo, &format!(\"{}::\", spec.rev))?;\n\n    // Descendants (newest first from jj), excluding anchor\n    let mut desc_lines: Vec<String> = Vec::new();\n    for commit_id in &descendant_ids {\n        if *commit_id == root_commit_id || *commit_id == *anchor_id {\n            continue;\n        }\n        let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;\n        desc_lines.push(format_commit(&commit, commit.change_id()));\n    }\n    if both_count > 0 {\n        let start = desc_lines.len().saturating_sub(both_count);\n        desc_lines = desc_lines[start..].to_vec();\n    } else {\n        desc_lines.clear();\n    }\n\n    // Anchor\n    let anchor_commit = repo.store().get_commit(anchor_id)?;\n\n    // Ancestors (newest first from jj), excluding anchor\n    let mut anc_lines: Vec<String> = Vec::new();\n    if both_count > 0 {\n        let mut count = 0;\n        for commit_id in &ancestor_ids {\n            if *commit_id == root_commit_id || *commit_id == *anchor_id {\n                continue;\n            }\n            let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;\n            anc_lines.push(format_commit(&commit, commit.change_id()));\n            count += 1;\n            if count >= both_count {\n                break;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    for line in &desc_lines {\n        println!(\"{line}\");\n    }\n    println!(\"{}\", format_commit(&anchor_commit, anchor_commit.change_id()));\n    for line in &anc_lines {\n        println!(\"{line}\");\n    }\n\n    Ok(())\n}"
}
```

    14→    pub revision: String,
    15→

    29→

    48→
    49→        count += 1;
    50→        if let Some(limit) = args.limit
    51→            && count >= limit
    52→        {
    53→            break;
    54→        }
    55→    }
    56→
    57→    Ok(())
    58→}

> AGENT

Three different formatting patterns:
- **chid**: just `change_short`
- **list**: `change_short commit_short first_line` (one line only)
- **desc**: `change_short commit_short first_line` + remaining lines (full description)

`list` is essentially `desc` but first-line only. I could move `format_commit` to `common.rs` and add a mode or a second helper. But chid is so different (just changeID) that it wouldn't benefit.

Simplest approach: move `format_commit` to `common.rs`, add a `format_commit_short` that returns just the first line (for `list`). Then `list` and `desc` both use common formatters. chid stays as-is.

Want me to do that as part of dev2, or keep it minimal and just do the version bump + desc?

> DEVELOPER

Let move it when we do list, it's working as is so let's commit if you think we're done

> AGENT

You're right. The "Finalize is running..." text is redundant — the bash output already shows the same info. And the 7 second delay to produce it means those trailing lines miss the finalize squash window, which is exactly the problem finalize's delay is supposed to solve.

Per CLAUDE.md: "End with only the status line — no other output." But the bash output *is* the status line. My echoing it again just creates trailing writes that get missed.

I should just run finalize and stop — no text output after it.

     1→# Memory
     2→
     3→## User Preferences
     4→
     5→- **Commit approval**: Always present the full commit commands to the user for physical review BEFORE executing them. Do not run jj commit / git commit until the user explicitly approves.
     6→- **Versioning**: Every plan must start with a version bump per notes/README.md "Versioning during development". Get user approval on single-step vs multi-step approach before starting.
     7→- **Pre-commit checklist**: Before committing, run: tests, clippy, fmt, install the app, then retest.
     8→
     9→## Feedback
    10→
    11→- [Always include ochid trailer in commits](feedback_ochid_trailer.md)
    12→- [Always commit .claude repo before finalize](feedback_claude_commit_before_finalize.md)
    13→- [Skip .claude push before finalize — finalize handles it](feedback_no_claude_push.md)
    14→- [Finalize must be absolute last action — only status line after](feedback_finalize_last.md)
    15→- [Use --ignore-immutable for late squash after push](feedback_squash_ignore_immutable.md)
    16→- [Use vc-x1 chid to retrieve changeIDs, not jj log](feedback_use_chid.md)
    17→- [Recognize when stuck — 5+ min thinking on simple task means stuck](feedback_stuck_detection.md)
    18→- [No --allow-new flag for jj git push](feedback_jj_no_allow_new.md)
    19→
    20→## Lessons Learned
    21→
    22→- **Avoid duplicate commits in .claude repo**: Committing the bot session repo multiple times in one session can create duplicate-titled commits with other session commits interleaved, making squashing non-trivial. Prefer a single commit to .claude at session end.
    23→- **ochid trailers need updating after squash**: When squashing changes the target commit in .claude, the ochid reference in the app repo must be updated to point at the correct changeID.
    24→- **Don't modify .claude repo programmatically mid-session**: Session data updates continuously, so bot-initiated changes to .claude can create circular modifications.
    25→
    26→## Key Files
    27→
    28→- notes/README.md — contains versioning workflow, todo format rules
    29→- notes/todo.md — near-term task tracking
    30→- notes/chores-01.md — chore details and reference links
    31→- CLAUDE.md — dual repo commit/push workflow
    32→

    11→**How to apply:** Do all memory writes, file edits, and other work BEFORE finalize. Then run finalize and output only the status line. If something unavoidably happens after finalize, run finalize again.

    30→    pub limit: Option<usize>,
    31→}
    32→
    33→fn format_commit(commit: &jj_lib::commit::Commit) -> String {
    34→    let change_hex = encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());
    35→    let change_short = &change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)];
    36→    let commit_hex = commit.id().hex();
    37→    let commit_short = &commit_hex[..commit_hex.len().min(12)];
    38→
    39→    let desc = commit.description();
    40→    if desc.is_empty() {
    41→        format!("{} {} (no description set)", change_short, commit_short)
    42→    } else {
    43→        let mut lines = desc.lines();
    44→        let first_line = lines.next().unwrap_or("");
    45→        let mut result = format!("{} {} {}", change_short, commit_short, first_line);
    46→        for line in lines {
    47→            result.push('\n');
    48→            result.push_str(line);
    49→        }
```

    21→    #[arg(short, long)]
    22→    pub limit: Option<usize>,
    23→}
    24→
    25→pub fn list(args: &ListArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
    26→    let (workspace, repo) = common::load_repo(&args.repo)?;
    27→
    28→    let commit_ids = common::resolve_revset(&workspace, &repo, &args.revision)?;
    29→
    30→    let root_commit_id = repo.store().root_commit_id().clone();
    31→
    32→    let mut count = 0;
    33→    for commit_id in &commit_ids {
    34→        if *commit_id == root_commit_id {
    35→            continue;
    36→        }
    37→        let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;
    38→
    39→        let change_hex = encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());
    40→        let change_short = &change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)];
    41→
    42→        let commit_hex = commit.id().hex();
    43→        let commit_short = &commit_hex[..commit_hex.len().min(12)];
    44→
    45→        let first_line = commit.description().lines().next().unwrap_or("");
    46→
    47→        println!("{} {} {}", change_short, commit_short, first_line);
    48→
    49→        count += 1;
    50→        if let Some(limit) = args.limit
    51→            && count >= limit
    52→        {
    53→            break;
    54→        }
    55→    }
    56→
    57→    Ok(())
    58→}

     1→use std::path::PathBuf;

     3→use clap::Args;
     4→use jj_lib::hex_util::encode_reverse_hex;
     5→use jj_lib::object_id::ObjectId;

     8→use crate::common;
     9→
    10→#[derive(Args, Debug)]
    11→pub struct ChidArgs {
    12→    /// Revision (with optional .. notation)
    13→    #[arg()]
    14→    pub pos_rev: Option<String>,
    15→
    16→    /// Count (items per dotted side)
    17→    #[arg()]
    18→    pub pos_count: Option<usize>,
    19→
    20→    /// Revision to look up (default: @)
    21→    #[arg(short, long, default_value = "@")]
    22→    pub revision: String,
    23→
    24→    /// Path to jj repo (default: current directory)
    25→    #[arg(short = 'R', long, default_value = ".")]
    26→    pub repo: PathBuf,
    27→
    28→    /// Maximum number of changeIDs to show
    29→    #[arg(short, long)]
    30→    pub limit: Option<usize>,
    31→}
    32→
    33→pub fn chid(args: &ChidArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
    34→    let resolved = common::resolve_dot_args(
    35→        args.pos_rev.as_deref(),
    36→        args.pos_count,
    37→        &args.revision,
    38→        args.limit,
    39→        "@",
    40→    );
    41→
    42→    let (workspace, repo) = common::load_repo(&args.repo)?;
    43→
    44→    let commit_ids = common::resolve_revset(&workspace, &repo, &resolved.revset)?;
    45→
    46→    if commit_ids.is_empty() {
    47→        return Err(format!("no commit found for revision '{}'", resolved.revset).into());
    48→    }
    49→
    50→    let root_commit_id = repo.store().root_commit_id().clone();
    51→
    52→    // For ..x.. (both), we need special handling: find x, take count ancestors + x + count descendants
    53→    if let Some(both_count) = resolved.both_count {
    54→        return print_both_chid(&repo, &root_commit_id, args, both_count);
    55→    }
    56→
    57→    let mut results: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
    58→    let mut count = 0;
    59→    for commit_id in &commit_ids {
    60→        if *commit_id == root_commit_id {
    61→            continue;
    62→        }
    63→        let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;
    64→        let change_hex = encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());
    65→        let change_short = &change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)];
    66→
    67→        results.push(change_short.to_string());
    68→
    69→        count += 1;
    70→        if let Some(limit) = resolved.limit
    71→            && count >= limit
    72→        {
    73→            break;
    74→        }
    75→    }
    76→
    77→    if resolved.reverse {
    78→        results.reverse();
    79→    }
    80→
    81→    for line in &results {
    82→        println!("{line}");
    83→    }
    84→
    85→    Ok(())
    86→}
    87→
    88→fn print_both_chid(
    89→    repo: &std::sync::Arc<jj_lib::repo::ReadonlyRepo>,
    90→    root_commit_id: &jj_lib::backend::CommitId,
    91→    args: &ChidArgs,
    92→    both_count: usize,
    93→) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
    94→    // We need to find the anchor revision to split ancestors/descendants.
    95→    // Re-resolve just the bare rev to find its commit ID.
    96→    let bare_rev = args.pos_rev.as_deref().unwrap_or("@");
    97→    let spec = common::parse_dot_rev(bare_rev);
    98→    let (workspace, repo2) = common::load_repo(&args.repo)?;
    99→    let anchor_ids = common::resolve_revset(&workspace, &repo2, &spec.rev)?;
   100→    if anchor_ids.is_empty() {
   101→        return Err(format!("no commit found for revision '{}'", spec.rev).into());
   102→    }
   103→    let anchor_id = &anchor_ids[0];
   104→
   105→    // Resolve ancestors (::x) and descendants (x::) separately
   106→    let ancestor_ids = common::resolve_revset(&workspace, repo, &format!("::{}", spec.rev))?;
   107→    let descendant_ids = common::resolve_revset(&workspace, repo, &format!("{}::", spec.rev))?;
   108→
   109→    // Descendants (newest first from jj), excluding anchor
   110→    let mut desc_lines: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
   111→    for commit_id in &descendant_ids {
   112→        if *commit_id == *root_commit_id || *commit_id == *anchor_id {
   113→            continue;
   114→        }
   115→        let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;
   116→        let change_hex = encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());
   117→        let change_short = &change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)];
   118→        desc_lines.push(change_short.to_string());
   119→    }
   120→    if both_count > 0 {
   121→        // Take the last `both_count` (closest to anchor), keep newest-first order
   122→        let start = desc_lines.len().saturating_sub(both_count);
   123→        desc_lines = desc_lines[start..].to_vec();
   124→    } else {
   125→        desc_lines.clear();
   126→    }
   127→
   128→    // Anchor
   129→    let anchor_commit = repo.store().get_commit(anchor_id)?;
   130→    let anchor_hex = encode_reverse_hex(anchor_commit.change_id().as_bytes());
   131→    let anchor_short = &anchor_hex[..anchor_hex.len().min(12)];
   132→
   133→    // Ancestors (newest first from jj), excluding anchor
   134→    let mut anc_lines: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
   135→    if both_count > 0 {
   136→        let mut count = 0;
   137→        for commit_id in &ancestor_ids {
   138→            if *commit_id == *root_commit_id || *commit_id == *anchor_id {
   139→                continue;
   140→            }
   141→            let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;
   142→            let change_hex = encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());
   143→            let change_short = &change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)];
   144→            anc_lines.push(change_short.to_string());
   145→            count += 1;
   146→            if count >= both_count {
   147→                break;
   148→            }
   149→        }
   150→    }
   151→
   152→    // Print: descendants (oldest first), then anchor, then ancestors (newest first)
   153→    for line in &desc_lines {
   154→        println!("{line}");
   155→    }
   156→    println!("{anchor_short}");
   157→    for line in &anc_lines {
   158→        println!("{line}");
   159→    }
   160→
   161→    Ok(())
   162→}
   163→
```

> AGENT

I see a lot of duplicated `print_both_*` logic across chid and desc (and list will need it too). Let me look at what can move to common.rs:

1. `format_commit` (full description) — used by desc
2. `format_commit_short` (first line only) — for list
3. `format_chid` (just changeID) — for chid
4. The `print_both` pattern — the iteration logic is identical, only the formatter differs

I'll move the formatters to common.rs, and add a generic `collect_both` helper that takes a formatter closure, eliminating the duplicated both-direction logic.

   190→}

   192→pub fn load_repo(
   193→    path: &Path,
   194→) -> Result<(Workspace, Arc<ReadonlyRepo>), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
   195→    let config = StackedConfig::with_defaults();
   196→    let settings = UserSettings::from_config(config)?;
   197→    let store_factories = StoreFactories::default();
   198→    let working_copy_factories = default_working_copy_factories();
   199→
   200→    let workspace = Workspace::load(&settings, path, &store_factories, &working_copy_factories)?;
   201→    let repo = workspace.repo_loader().load_at_head().block_on()?;
   202→
   203→    Ok((workspace, repo))
   204→}
   205→
   206→/// Parse and evaluate a revset string, returning commit IDs.
   207→pub fn resolve_revset(
   208→    workspace: &Workspace,
   209→    repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>,
   210→    revset_str: &str,
   211→) -> Result<Vec<CommitId>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
   212→    let aliases_map = RevsetAliasesMap::new();
   213→    let fileset_aliases_map = FilesetAliasesMap::new();
   214→    let extensions = RevsetExtensions::default();
   215→    let workspace_root = workspace.workspace_root().to_path_buf();
   216→    let path_converter = RepoPathUiConverter::Fs {
   217→        cwd: workspace_root.clone(),
   218→        base: workspace_root,
   219→    };
   220→    let workspace_name = workspace.workspace_name();
   221→    let workspace_ctx = RevsetWorkspaceContext {
   222→        path_converter: &path_converter,
   223→        workspace_name,
   224→    };
   225→    let context = RevsetParseContext {
   226→        aliases_map: &aliases_map,
   227→        local_variables: HashMap::new(),
   228→        user_email: "",
   229→        date_pattern_context: chrono::Utc::now().fixed_offset().into(),
   230→        default_ignored_remote: None,
   231→        fileset_aliases_map: &fileset_aliases_map,
   232→        use_glob_by_default: false,
   233→        extensions: &extensions,
   234→        workspace: Some(workspace_ctx),
   235→    };
   236→
   237→    let mut diagnostics = RevsetDiagnostics::new();
   238→    let expression = jj_lib::revset::parse(&mut diagnostics, revset_str, &context)?;
   239→
   240→    let no_extensions: &[Box<dyn jj_lib::revset::SymbolResolverExtension>] = &[];
   241→    let symbol_resolver = SymbolResolver::new(repo.as_ref(), no_extensions);
   242→    let resolved = expression.resolve_user_expression(repo.as_ref(), &symbol_resolver)?;
   243→    let revset = resolved.evaluate(repo.as_ref())?;
   244→
   245→    let mut commit_ids = Vec::new();
   246→    for result in revset.iter() {
   247→        commit_ids.push(result?);
   248→    }
   249→    Ok(commit_ids)
   250→}
   251→

   253→mod tests {
   254→    use super::*;

encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());\n    let change_short = &change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)];\n    let commit_hex = commit.id().hex();\n    let commit_short = &commit_hex[..commit_hex.len().min(12)];\n    let first_line = commit.description().lines().next().unwrap_or(\"\");\n    if first_line.is_empty() {\n        format!(\"{change_short} {commit_short} (no description set)\")\n    } else {\n        format!(\"{change_short} {commit_short} {first_line}\")\n    }\n}\n\n/// Format: changeID commitID first-line, then remaining description lines.\npub fn format_commit_full(commit: &Commit) -> String {\n    let change_hex = encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());\n    let change_short = &change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)];\n    let commit_hex = commit.id().hex();\n    let commit_short = &commit_hex[..commit_hex.len().min(12)];\n    let desc = commit.description();\n    if desc.is_empty() {\n        format!(\"{change_short} {commit_short} (no description set)\")\n    } else {\n        let mut lines = desc.lines();\n        let first_line = lines.next().unwrap_or(\"\");\n        let mut result = format!(\"{change_short} {commit_short} {first_line}\");\n        for line in lines {\n            result.push('\\n');\n            result.push_str(line);\n        }\n        result\n    }\n}\n\n/// Collect formatted lines for `..x..` (both directions).\n///\n/// `formatter` converts a `Commit` into a display string.\n/// Returns the lines in display order: descendants (newest first),\n/// anchor, ancestors (newest first).\npub fn collect_both<F>(\n    repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>,\n    repo_path: &Path,\n    pos_rev: Option<&str>,\n    both_count: usize,\n    formatter: F,\n) -> Result<Vec<String>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>>\nwhere\n    F: Fn(&Commit) -> String,\n{\n    let bare_rev = pos_rev.unwrap_or(\"@\");\n    let spec = parse_dot_rev(bare_rev);\n    let (workspace, _) = load_repo(repo_path)?;\n    let anchor_ids = resolve_revset(&workspace, repo, &spec.rev)?;\n    if anchor_ids.is_empty() {\n        return Err(format!(\"no commit found for revision '{}'\", spec.rev).into());\n    }\n    let anchor_id = &anchor_ids[0];\n    let root_commit_id = repo.store().root_commit_id().clone();\n\n    let ancestor_ids = resolve_revset(&workspace, repo, &format!(\"::{}\", spec.rev))?;\n    let descendant_ids = resolve_revset(&workspace, repo, &format!(\"{}::\", spec.rev))?;\n\n    // Descendants (newest first from jj), excluding anchor\n    let mut desc_lines: Vec<String> = Vec::new();\n    for commit_id in &descendant_ids {\n        if *commit_id == root_commit_id || *commit_id == *anchor_id {\n            continue;\n        }\n        let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;\n        desc_lines.push(formatter(&commit));\n    }\n    if both_count > 0 {\n        let start = desc_lines.len().saturating_sub(both_count);\n        desc_lines = desc_lines[start..].to_vec();\n    } else {\n        desc_lines.clear();\n    }\n\n    // Anchor\n    let anchor_commit = repo.store().get_commit(anchor_id)?;\n\n    // Ancestors (newest first from jj), excluding anchor\n    let mut anc_lines: Vec<String> = Vec::new();\n    if both_count > 0 {\n        let mut count = 0;\n        for commit_id in &ancestor_ids {\n            if root_commit_id == *commit_id || *commit_id == *anchor_id {\n                continue;\n            }\n            let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;\n            anc_lines.push(formatter(&commit));\n            count += 1;\n            if count >= both_count {\n                break;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    let mut result = desc_lines;\n    result.push(formatter(&anchor_commit));\n    result.extend(anc_lines);\n    Ok(result)\n}\n\npub fn load_repo("
}

   #[arg()]\n    pub pos_rev: Option<String>,\n\n    /// Count (items per dotted side)\n    #[arg()]\n    pub pos_count: Option<usize>,\n\n    /// Revision to look up (default: @)\n    #[arg(short, long, default_value = \"@\")]\n    pub revision: String,\n\n    /// Path to jj repo (default: current directory)\n    #[arg(short = 'R', long, default_value = \".\")]\n    pub repo: PathBuf,\n\n    /// Maximum number of changeIDs to show\n    #[arg(short, long)]\n    pub limit: Option<usize>,\n}\n\npub fn chid(args: &ChidArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let resolved = common::resolve_dot_args(\n        args.pos_rev.as_deref(),\n        args.pos_count,\n        &args.revision,\n        args.limit,\n        \"@\",\n    );\n\n    let (workspace, repo) = common::load_repo(&args.repo)?;\n\n    let commit_ids = common::resolve_revset(&workspace, &repo, &resolved.revset)?;\n\n    if commit_ids.is_empty() {\n        return Err(format!(\"no commit found for revision '{}'\", resolved.revset).into());\n    }\n\n    let root_commit_id = repo.store().root_commit_id().clone();\n\n    if let Some(both_count) = resolved.both_count {\n        let lines =\n            common::collect_both(&repo, &args.repo, args.pos_rev.as_deref(), both_count, common::format_chid)?;\n        for line in &lines {\n            println!(\"{line}\");\n        }\n        return Ok(());\n    }\n\n    let mut results: Vec<String> = Vec::new();\n    let mut count = 0;\n    for commit_id in &commit_ids {\n        if *commit_id == root_commit_id {\n            continue;\n        }\n        let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;\n        results.push(common::format_chid(&commit));\n\n        count += 1;\n        if let Some(limit) = resolved.limit\n            && count >= limit\n        {\n            break;\n        }\n    }\n\n    for line in &results {\n        println!(\"{line}\");\n    }\n\n    Ok(())\n}\n"
}

common::load_repo(&args.repo)?;\n\n    let commit_ids = common::resolve_revset(&workspace, &repo, &resolved.revset)?;\n\n    if commit_ids.is_empty() {\n        return Err(format!(\"no commit found for revision '{}'\", resolved.revset).into());\n    }\n\n    let root_commit_id = repo.store().root_commit_id().clone();\n\n    if let Some(both_count) = resolved.both_count {\n        let lines = common::collect_both(\n            &repo,\n            &args.repo,\n            args.pos_rev.as_deref(),\n            both_count,\n            common::format_commit_full,\n        )?;\n        for line in &lines {\n            println!(\"{line}\");\n        }\n        return Ok(());\n    }\n\n    let mut results: Vec<String> = Vec::new();\n    let mut count = 0;\n    for commit_id in &commit_ids {\n        if *commit_id == root_commit_id {\n            continue;\n        }\n        let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;\n        results.push(common::format_commit_full(&commit));\n\n        count += 1;\n        if let Some(limit) = resolved.limit\n            && count >= limit\n        {\n            break;\n        }\n    }\n\n    for line in &results {\n        println!(\"{line}\");\n    }\n\n    Ok(())\n}\n"
}

   #[arg()]\n    pub pos_rev: Option<String>,\n\n    /// Count (items per dotted side)\n    #[arg()]\n    pub pos_count: Option<usize>,\n\n    /// Revision to list (default: @)\n    #[arg(short, long, default_value = \"@\")]\n    pub revision: String,\n\n    /// Path to jj repo (default: current directory)\n    #[arg(short = 'R', long, default_value = \".\")]\n    pub repo: PathBuf,\n\n    /// Maximum number of commits to show\n    #[arg(short, long)]\n    pub limit: Option<usize>,\n}\n\npub fn list(args: &ListArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let resolved = common::resolve_dot_args(\n        args.pos_rev.as_deref(),\n        args.pos_count,\n        &args.revision,\n        args.limit,\n        \"@\",\n    );\n\n    let (workspace, repo) = common::load_repo(&args.repo)?;\n\n    let commit_ids = common::resolve_revset(&workspace, &repo, &resolved.revset)?;\n\n    if commit_ids.is_empty() {\n        return Err(format!(\"no commit found for revision '{}'\", resolved.revset).into());\n    }\n\n    let root_commit_id = repo.store().root_commit_id().clone();\n\n    if let Some(both_count) = resolved.both_count {\n        let lines = common::collect_both(\n            &repo,\n            &args.repo,\n            args.pos_rev.as_deref(),\n            both_count,\n            common::format_commit_short,\n        )?;\n        for line in &lines {\n            println!(\"{line}\");\n        }\n        return Ok(());\n    }\n\n    let mut results: Vec<String> = Vec::new();\n    let mut count = 0;\n    for commit_id in &commit_ids {\n        if *commit_id == root_commit_id {\n            continue;\n        }\n        let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;\n        results.push(common::format_commit_short(&commit));\n\n        count += 1;\n        if let Some(limit) = resolved.limit\n            && count >= limit\n        {\n            break;\n        }\n    }\n\n    for line in &results {\n        println!(\"{line}\");\n    }\n\n    Ok(())\n}\n"
