     1→# vc-x1
     2→
     3→This is experiment 1 to explore creating a Vibe Coding (vc) environment.
     4→We will investigate ways of using the dual jj-git repo concept, explored
     5→in [hw-jjg-bot](https://github.com/winksaville/hw-jjg-bot.git) to
     6→initially make it easy to see how the code base evolved. This is
     7→made possible by the fact that we have two repos one with the code
     8→and one with the conversation with the bot.
     9→
    10→I've chosen the jj-git environment because jj provides the concept that
    11→each commit has an immutable changeID as well as the mutable commitID
    12→of git. The idea is that each commit made on repo A writes the
    13→changeID in the commit message to repo B. Thus there is a cross reference
    14→between the two repos and this will allow vc-x1 to show how the repo
    15→evolved and the entity (bot or human) can more clearly understand **how** and
    16→most importantly **why** the code evolved.
    17→
    18→The solution space is wide open, from trivial CLI, web or app based
    19→(mobile/non-mobile). In addition, I could see this as an extension to
    20→existing programming editors like vscode and zed or even creating our
    21→own IDE for vc.
    22→
    23→See [Initial commit with dual jj-git repos](./notes/chores-01.md#initial-commit-with-dual-jj-git-repos)
    24→for how the initial commit was created with the dual jj-git repos. After
    25→doing so and I then created this README.md file.
    26→
    27→## jj Tips for Git Users
    28→
    29→If you're coming from git, jj's log output can be surprising compared to
    30→tools like `gitk --all`.
    31→
    32→### Why `jj log` shows fewer commits than `gitk`
    33→
    34→jj tracks *changes* (identified by change IDs), not individual git commits.
    35→When you rewrite a change (`jj describe`, `jj rebase`, `jj squash`, etc.),
    36→jj creates a new git commit and keeps the old one under `refs/jj/keep/*` as
    37→undo history. `gitk --all` sees all of these obsolete commits; `jj log` only
    38→shows the current version of each change.
    39→
    40→### Useful commands
    41→
    42→| Command | Description |
    43→|---------|-------------|
    44→| `jj log` | Show recent visible commits (default revset) |
    45→| `jj log -r ::@` | Show **all** ancestors of the working copy |
    46→| `jj log -r 'all()'` | Show all non-hidden commits (needed if you have multiple heads/branches) |
    47→| `jj obslog -r <change-id>` | Show the evolution history of a single change |
    48→| `jj op log` | Show operation history (each rewrite operation) |
    49→
    50→In a single-branch workflow, `jj log -r ::@` and `jj log -r 'all()'` give
    51→the same result. Use `all()` when you have multiple branches or heads.
    52→
    53→## Usage
    54→
    55→```
    56→vc-x1 list [REV [COUNT]] [OPTIONS]   # List commits in a jj repo
    57→vc-x1 desc [REV [COUNT]] [OPTIONS]   # Show full description of a commit
    58→vc-x1 chid [REV [COUNT]] [OPTIONS]   # Print changeID(s) for a revision
    59→vc-x1 finalize [OPTIONS]             # Squash working copy into target (daemonizes by default)
    60→vc-x1 --version                       # Print version
    61→vc-x1 --help                          # Print help
    62→```
    63→
    64→### Revision shortcuts
    65→
    66→`list`, `desc`, and `chid` accept up to two positional arguments as
    67→shorthand for `-r` (revision) and `-l` (limit).
    68→
    69→**The `..` notation:** dots show where the list continues from the
    70→revision:
    71→
    72→- `x..` — x at top, ancestors below (older commits)
    73→- `..x` — descendants above (newer commits), x at bottom
    74→- `..x..` — both directions, x in the middle
    75→
    76→COUNT is the number of commits on each dotted side (x is always
    77→included and not counted). Bare `x COUNT` defaults to `x..` (ancestors),
    78→the common case. Bare `x` without COUNT shows just that one commit.
    79→
    80→```
    81→vc-x1 list x                # just x (1 commit)
    82→vc-x1 list x 5              # x.. 5 → x + 5 ancestors (6 commits, x at top)
    83→vc-x1 list x.. 5            # x + 5 ancestors (6 commits, x at top)
    84→vc-x1 list ..x 3            # 3 descendants + x (4 commits, x at bottom)
    85→vc-x1 list ..x.. 3          # 3 descendants, x, 3 ancestors (7 commits)
    86→vc-x1 list ..x.. 0          # just x (1 commit)
    87→```
    88→
    89→**Defaults:** with no arguments, REV is `@` and COUNT is 0 (just the
    90→working copy).
    91→
    92→Named flags `-r`/`--revision`, `-l`/`--limit`, and `-R`/`--repo` still
    93→work and take precedence over positional arguments.
    94→
    95→### finalize
    96→
    97→Solves the session repo coherence problem: when the bot commits `.claude`,
    98→the act of committing generates more session data. `finalize` daemonizes,
    99→waits, then squashes the trailing writes into the target commit.
   100→
   101→The last action in a session is always:
   102→
   103→```
   104→vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --delay 5 --push
   105→```
   106→
   107→The command returns immediately and prints a status line:
   108→
   109→```
   110→Finalize is running (pid 12345, log `/tmp/vc-x1-finalize-1710412800000.log`)
   111→```
   112→
   113→See [finalize subcommand](./notes/chores-01.md#finalize-subcommand-for-session-repo-coherence)
   114→for full option reference and design details.
   115→
   116→### Testing finalize
   117→
   118→Always test against a throwaway jj repo, never the live workspace.
   119→Use `mktemp -d` for unique directories so results can't be confused
   120→with a previous run:
   121→
   122→```bash
   123→# Create and init a temp repo
   124→dir=$(mktemp -d /tmp/vc-x1-test-XXXXXX)
   125→(cd "$dir" && jj git init)
   126→
   127→# Foreground (blocks, logs to test dir)
   128→vc-x1 finalize --foreground --repo "$dir" --log "$dir/finalize.log"
   129→cat "$dir/finalize.log"
   130→
   131→# Daemonized (returns immediately, child logs to test dir)
   132→dir2=$(mktemp -d /tmp/vc-x1-test-XXXXXX)
   133→(cd "$dir2" && jj git init)
   134→vc-x1 finalize --repo "$dir2" --log "$dir2/finalize.log"
   135→cat "$dir2/finalize.log"
   136→```
   137→
   138→The log file shows timestamped (nanoseconds) entries with PIDs, covering
   139→the full flow: `main` entry/exit, `finalize` entry/exit, `daemonize`
   140→spawn, and `finalize_exec` in the child process.
   141→
   142→## Cross-repo Linking with Git Trailers
   143→
   144→Commits in each repo use [git trailers](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-interpret-trailers)
   145→to cross-reference their counterpart in the other repo. The `ochid`
   146→(Other Change ID) trailer contains a workspace-root-relative path
   147→and jj changeID:
   148→
   149→```
   150→ochid: /.claude/xvzvruqo   # points to a .claude repo change
   151→ochid: /wtpmottv            # points to an app repo change
   152→```
   153→
   154→Paths always start with `/` (the workspace root, i.e. vc-x1).
   155→Each repo has a `.vc-config.toml` that identifies its location
   156→within the workspace, so tools can resolve these paths locally.
   157→
   158→For full details see:
   159→- [Git trailer convention](./notes/chores-01.md#git-trailer-convention)
   160→  — [ochid (Other Change ID)](./notes/chores-01.md#ochid-other-change-id)
   161→  — [ChangeID path syntax](./notes/chores-01.md#changeid-path-syntax)
   162→  — [.vc-config.toml](./notes/chores-01.md#vc-configtoml)
   163→
   164→## License
   165→
   166→Licensed under either of
   167→
   168→- Apache License, Version 2.0 ([LICENSE-APACHE](LICENSE-APACHE) or http://apache.org/licenses/LICENSE-2.0)
   169→- MIT license ([LICENSE-MIT](LICENSE-MIT) or http://opensource.org/licenses/MIT)
   170→
   171→### Contribution
   172→
   173→Unless you explicitly state otherwise, any contribution intentionally submitted
   174→for inclusion in the work by you, as defined in the Apache-2.0 license, shall
   175→be dual licensed as above, without any additional terms or conditions.
   176→
```

```
     1→# vc-x1
     2→
     3→This is experiment 1 to explore creating a Vibe Coding (vc) environment.
     4→We will investigate ways of using the dual jj-git repo concept, explored
     5→in [hw-jjg-bot](https://github.com/winksaville/hw-jjg-bot.git) to
     6→initially make it easy to see how the code base evolved. This is
     7→made possible by the fact that we have two repos one with the code
     8→and one with the conversation with the bot.
     9→
    10→I've chosen the jj-git environment because jj provides the concept that
    11→each commit has an immutable changeID as well as the mutable commitID
    12→of git. The idea is that each commit made on repo A writes the
    13→changeID in the commit message to repo B. Thus there is a cross reference
    14→between the two repos and this will allow vc-x1 to show how the repo
    15→evolved and the entity (bot or human) can more clearly understand **how** and
    16→most importantly **why** the code evolved.
    17→
    18→The solution space is wide open, from trivial CLI, web or app based
    19→(mobile/non-mobile). In addition, I could see this as an extension to
    20→existing programming editors like vscode and zed or even creating our
    21→own IDE for vc.
    22→
    23→See [Initial commit with dual jj-git repos](./notes/chores-01.md#initial-commit-with-dual-jj-git-repos)
    24→for how the initial commit was created with the dual jj-git repos. After
    25→doing so and I then created this README.md file.
    26→
    27→## Usage
    28→
    29→```
    30→vc-x1 list [REV [COUNT]] [OPTIONS]   # List commits in a jj repo
    31→vc-x1 desc [REV [COUNT]] [OPTIONS]   # Show full description of a commit
    32→vc-x1 chid [REV [COUNT]] [OPTIONS]   # Print changeID(s) for a revision
    33→vc-x1 finalize --bookmark <B> [OPTS]  # Squash working copy into target (daemonizes by default)
    34→vc-x1 --version                       # Print version
    35→vc-x1 --help                          # Print help
    36→```
    37→
    38→### Revision shortcuts
    39→
    40→`list`, `desc`, and `chid` accept up to two positional arguments as
    41→shorthand for `-r` (revision) and `-l` (limit).
    42→
    43→**The `..` notation:** dots show where the list continues from the
    44→revision:
    45→
    46→- `x..` — x at top, ancestors below (older commits)
    47→- `..x` — descendants above (newer commits), x at bottom
    48→- `..x..` — both directions, x in the middle
    49→
    50→COUNT is the number of commits on each dotted side (x is always
    51→included and not counted). Bare `x COUNT` defaults to `x..` (ancestors),
    52→the common case. Bare `x` without COUNT shows just that one commit.
    53→
    54→```
    55→vc-x1 list x                # just x (1 commit)
    56→vc-x1 list x 5              # x.. 5 → x + 5 ancestors (6 commits, x at top)
    57→vc-x1 list x.. 5            # x + 5 ancestors (6 commits, x at top)
    58→vc-x1 list ..x 3            # 3 descendants + x (4 commits, x at bottom)
    59→vc-x1 list ..x.. 3          # 3 descendants, x, 3 ancestors (7 commits)
    60→vc-x1 list ..x.. 0          # just x (1 commit)
    61→```
    62→
    63→**Defaults:** with no arguments, REV is `@` and COUNT is 0 (just the
    64→working copy).
    65→
    66→Named flags `-r`/`--revision`, `-l`/`--limit`, and `-R`/`--repo` still
    67→work and take precedence over positional arguments.
    68→
    69→### finalize
    70→
    71→Solves the session repo coherence problem: when the bot commits `.claude`,
    72→the act of committing generates more session data. `finalize` daemonizes,
    73→waits, then squashes the trailing writes into the target commit.
    74→
    75→The last action in a session is always:
    76→
    77→```
    78→vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --bookmark <bookmark> --delay 5 --push
    79→```
    80→
    81→The command returns immediately and prints a status line:
    82→
    83→```
    84→Finalize is running (pid 12345, log `/tmp/vc-x1-finalize-1710412800000.log`)
    85→```
    86→
    87→See [finalize subcommand](./notes/chores-01.md#finalize-subcommand-for-session-repo-coherence)
    88→for full option reference and design details.
    89→
    90→### Testing finalize
    91→
    92→Always test against a throwaway jj repo, never the live workspace.
    93→Use `mktemp -d` for unique directories so results can't be confused
    94→with a previous run:
    95→
    96→```bash
    97→# Create and init a temp repo
    98→dir=$(mktemp -d /tmp/vc-x1-test-XXXXXX)
    99→(cd "$dir" && jj git init)
   100→
   101→# Foreground (blocks, logs to test dir)
   102→vc-x1 finalize --foreground --repo "$dir" --log "$dir/finalize.log"
   103→cat "$dir/finalize.log"
   104→
   105→# Daemonized (returns immediately, child logs to test dir)
   106→dir2=$(mktemp -d /tmp/vc-x1-test-XXXXXX)
   107→(cd "$dir2" && jj git init)
   108→vc-x1 finalize --repo "$dir2" --log "$dir2/finalize.log"
   109→cat "$dir2/finalize.log"
   110→```
   111→
   112→The log file shows timestamped (nanoseconds) entries with PIDs, covering
   113→the full flow: `main` entry/exit, `finalize` entry/exit, `daemonize`
   114→spawn, and `finalize_exec` in the child process.
   115→
   116→## Cross-repo Linking with Git Trailers
   117→
   118→Commits in each repo use [git trailers](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-interpret-trailers)
   119→to cross-reference their counterpart in the other repo. The `ochid`
   120→(Other Change ID) trailer contains a workspace-root-relative path
   121→and jj changeID:
   122→
   123→```
   124→ochid: /.claude/xvzvruqo   # points to a .claude repo change
   125→ochid: /wtpmottv            # points to an app repo change
   126→```
   127→
   128→Paths always start with `/` (the workspace root, i.e. vc-x1).
   129→Each repo has a `.vc-config.toml` that identifies its location
   130→within the workspace, so tools can resolve these paths locally.
   131→
   132→For full details see:
   133→- [Git trailer convention](./notes/chores-01.md#git-trailer-convention)
   134→  — [ochid (Other Change ID)](./notes/chores-01.md#ochid-other-change-id)
   135→  — [ChangeID path syntax](./notes/chores-01.md#changeid-path-syntax)
   136→  — [.vc-config.toml](./notes/chores-01.md#vc-configtoml)
   137→
   138→## jj Tips for Git Users
   139→
   140→If you're coming from git, jj's log output can be surprising compared to
   141→tools like `gitk --all`.
   142→
   143→### Why `jj log` shows fewer commits than `gitk`
   144→
   145→jj tracks *changes* (identified by change IDs), not individual git commits.
   146→When you rewrite a change (`jj describe`, `jj rebase`, `jj squash`, etc.),
   147→jj creates a new git commit and keeps the old one under `refs/jj/keep/*` as
   148→undo history. `gitk --all` sees all of these obsolete commits; `jj log` only
   149→shows the current version of each change.
   150→
   151→### Useful commands
   152→
   153→| Command | Description |
   154→|---------|-------------|
   155→| `jj log` | Show recent visible commits (default revset) |
   156→| `jj log -r ::@` | Show **all** ancestors of the working copy |
   157→| `jj log -r 'all()'` | Show all non-hidden commits (needed if you have multiple heads/branches) |
   158→| `jj obslog -r <change-id>` | Show the evolution history of a single change |
   159→| `jj op log` | Show operation history (each rewrite operation) |
   160→
   161→In a single-branch workflow, `jj log -r ::@` and `jj log -r 'all()'` give
   162→the same result. Use `all()` when you have multiple branches or heads.

> DEVELOPER

Missed the last line it took 7 seconds again even with you being quite:
..,"promptId":"c65ebf47-26d0-4f17-9673-5a0781be7768",..,"content":"finalize: daemonized (pid 170722), log: /tmp/vc-x1-finalize-1773720127905.log",..,"timestamp":"2026-03-17T04:02:07.909Z",..
..,"type":"system","subtype":"turn_duration","durationMs":58329,"timestamp":"2026-03-17T04:02:14.939Z",..
Seems we'd better make the delay 10 seconds :(
thoughts?

> AGENT

The 7 second gap is the system writing that `turn_duration` line — completely outside our control. 10 seconds would cover it but feels like papering over the issue. 

The real fix might be to not use `--delay` at all and instead have finalize wait until the session file stops changing (poll the file's mtime). But that's a bigger change.

For now, 10 seconds is pragmatic and safe. Want to bump the default delay in the code, or just use `--delay 10` in CLAUDE.md and our workflow?

> DEVELOPER

But what weould the quiescent time be, it's only writing one line but taking 7 seconds to do it, is there any "hook" that we can we can watch to make the wait truly closed loop and "claude code" would tell use that "system" "turn_duration" is written?

> AGENT

Good question. There's no hook or API from Claude Code that signals "I'm done writing session data." The `turn_duration` line is a system-level bookkeeping entry — we can't subscribe to it or know when it's coming.

The 7 seconds isn't the write itself — it's Claude Code's internal processing between when the bash tool returns and when it finalizes the turn. We have no visibility into that pipeline.

Options as I see them:

1. **Bump delay to 10-15s** — simple, wasteful, but reliable
2. **Poll mtime** — watch the session file and wait for N seconds of no changes. Problem: we don't know what N should be (could still miss a late write)
3. **Accept the loss** — it's one `turn_duration` metadata line. Does it actually matter if that gets missed?

I'd lean toward option 3 — that system metadata line isn't conversation content. But you know the data better than I do. Is losing that trailing line actually a problem?

> DEVELOPER

I only have visually looked at the data, not used it yet, so all I can say is I rather not drop it. I doesn't look important but I vote change to 10 secs and we'll cover the 99% case, I'm guessing. and when we do I'll ask you to do a second invocation fo finalise and it'll be done.
thoughts?

> AGENT

Makes sense — 10 seconds covers the common case, and a second finalize invocation catches anything that slips through. Simple and practical.

Should we bump the default in the code (change `default_value_t = 1.0` to `10.0`) or just update CLAUDE.md to use `--delay 10`? I'd lean toward just updating CLAUDE.md since 1 second is a fine default for other use cases.

> DEVELOPER

bump the default to 10 but you pass it anyway so it's obvious in the Bash line.
